I just haven't had much of an appetite, that's really all it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling stressed or overwhelmed lately?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Everything physically seems pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in eating is related to anything going on in your life or your thoughts?\n\nPatient: I suppose it could be. I mean, I don't have a lot going on, but most days lately I just wake up with this... heavy, flat feeling. It's kind of hard to explain, but it's like the apartment is just too quiet. I haven't been dieting or trying to slim down or anything like that. It's really just that... I don't know, I just don't have any desire to eat, so it's just kind of happening on its own.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious, depressed, or upset when you think about eating?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, food doesn't make me upset or anxious or anything like that. It's more just... I just don't feel hungry. There's no real emotion attached to it, just a lack of interest, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in interest in activities you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, I still like doing the things I usually do... I haven't lost interest in my hobbies or anything. It's really just the food thing that's been weird lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are at a healthy weight right now?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I've probably dropped a few pounds recently just from not eating as much, but... I don't feel like I'm underweight or anything like that. I'd say I'm still at a pretty normal weight.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you look too fat, even if you've lost weight recently?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I don't think I look fat. I mean, I know I've lost a little weight, but I don't have any issues with how my body looks. It's just... I just haven't had much of an appetite, that's really all it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling stressed or overwhelmed lately?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I don't have a whole lot on my plate right now, so there's not much to be stressed about. I definitely wouldn't say I've been feeling overwhelmed or anything like that. Things have been pretty quiet.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any physical health problems lately?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Physically, I feel fine. I mean, other than just not having much of an appetite like I mentioned, there's nothing wrong. No aches or pains or anything like that. Everything physically seems pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in eating is related to anything going on in your life or your thoughts?\n\nPatient: I suppose it could be. I mean, I don't have a lot going on, but most days lately I just wake up with this... heavy, flat feeling. It's kind of hard to explain, but it's like the apartment is just too quiet. Usually, I'd play my guitar or something, but lately even picking that up doesn't really lift me. So I guess when I'm feeling like that, eating just doesn't really cross my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or empty most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess \"empty\" is a pretty good word for it. Like I was just saying, it's that flat, heavy feeling I wake up with. And yeah, it pretty much sticks around for most of the day. It's just hard to shake it off, even when I try to do something I usually like, like playing my guitar.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're worthless or guilty for no reason?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't think I feel worthless... and I definitely don't feel guilty about anything. It's really just that flat, empty feeling I was talking about. I'm not really beating myself up or anything like that, I just kind of feel blank.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have less energy than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. It's just... I don't really have the drive to do much. Like I said, even picking up my guitar feels like too much effort most days. I just kind of sit around the apartment. So, yeah, definitely less energy.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little bit. I mean, I am sleeping, but it’s just really broken up. I keep waking up around four or five in the morning, and my mind just goes straight to things like bills, or... you know, unfinished tracks I haven't touched. Once I'm up thinking about all that, it's hard to settle back down. So I end up feeling tired before the day even really starts.\n\nDoctor: How much weight would you say you've lost since your eating changed?\n\nPatient: I haven't actually stepped on a scale, so I don't know the exact number of pounds, but my jeans are definitely starting to feel loose. For the past few weeks, my eating has just been off. I’ll end up skipping breakfast and lunch without really meaning to, and then... I just kind of pick at dinner, even when my wife cooks.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in eating is related to this empty, flat feeling you've been having?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think they're connected. Most days lately, I just wake up with this... this heavy, flat feeling. It's like the apartment is just too quiet, but I don't really want to do anything about it. Usually playing music helps, but lately even picking up my guitar doesn’t really lift me out of it. So when I feel that heavy, I just don't really care about food either.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant loss of pleasure in activities other than eating over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, like I mentioned with the guitar, that's the main thing. Other than that... I guess I haven't really been doing much else to even know. I'm just mostly hanging around the apartment right now. So, no, there isn't really a specific list of other things I've stopped enjoying... it's just that general flat feeling with whatever I do.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost more than 5% of your body weight over the past month?\n\nPatient: I really couldn't give you an exact percentage since I haven't been on a scale. But like I mentioned earlier, it's definitely noticeable. With my eating being so off these past few weeks—skipping breakfast and lunch, and just picking at the dinner my wife makes—I've definitely dropped enough weight that my jeans are fitting loose. So, yeah, it could be around five percent, I'm just not totally sure on the exact number.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
